# Core Set II Art-Bible Drafts

> **DRAFT (2026-10-09).** This file holds the set register, 140 creature entries and 110 spell and permanent prompts for the 250 cards; drafting questions are collected at the end under Flags from drafting. They are the cards the owner agreed in the Core Set II cut. Card data does not exist yet: transcription follows the art run (`docs/plan-core-set-2.md`, wave 3). So, like the Sands of the Duat drafts, it sits outside `check-art-bible`. Its **Card facts** lines are transcribed from the overplan working copy, not from `src/data/`. Costs are the provisional 25-life rescore, and the scorer's life terms are re-derived in D3, so a cost can still move. When the cards are transcribed, the set register below is promoted to `index.md` as section 4e, the creature entries move to `core-set-2.md` (where the checker covers them), and the spell and permanent prompts move to `docs/spell-art.md`. Card ids here use the overplan's `cs2-` working prefix, and the final prefix is set at transcription. Every character is an adult woman.

## Set register (proposed section 4e of `index.md`)

This register applies to every Core Set II art-bible entry, to every spell-art entry for the set, and to every set-scoped key visual. The global rules in `index.md` sections 2 to 6 remain in force. Its source is section 14 of the identity brief.

**The Mandate has fallen.** The sign of the right to rule has come loose from heaven. It can be held, carried into battle, and taken by whoever strikes its keeper, and every realm reaches for it:

- **The Three Kingdoms** fight for it as they always have.
- **Olympus** claims the right to grant it, and swears its oaths on the Styx.
- **The Beastkin** of the wilds raid whoever holds it.

**The tone is the homecoming.** The base set's look returns with a larger cast and a sharper eye: lacquer and marble, crimson and gold, banners, river-fleets, temples on cliffs. It is elegant, glamorous and adult, grand rather than grim, and lit warmer than the base set. The register's job is to make the old houses look like the best version of themselves, and to make one small object, the Mandate's sign, readable in a 216 px window.

### Accent palette

Layer these accents over the card's colour-identity palette (section 4), the way the faction accents already work. The base set's faction accents stand:

- Wei: lapis and bronze.
- Wu: crimson and river-gold.
- Shu: jade and ivory.
- Jin: slate-teal and tarnished silver.
- Other Three Kingdoms: black iron and ember.
- Greek: marble, Aegean blue and gilt.
- Beastkin: warm earth, moss and fur naturals.

The new accents below are the homecoming's shared vocabulary.

| Accent | Hex | Required visual use |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Lacquer red** | `#b3202a` / `#e0473c` | lacquered armour, pillars, banners, saddle leather; the Three Kingdoms' signature red |
| **Imperial gold** | `#d4a83a` / `#f5d77a` | trims, bronze-gilt fittings, palace roofs, laurel gilt |
| **Jade** | `#3f9a78` / `#8fd3b0` | jade ornaments, Shu green, bamboo, jade-stream water |
| **River blue** | `#2f6fa8` / `#7fb3de` | the Yangtze and the river-fleets, Wu's water, evening sky |
| **Marble white** | `#f4f1ea` / `#d9d3c5` | temples, columns, chitons, Olympus's clouds |
| **Laurel green** | `#6b8f3e` / `#a9c46e` | laurel, olive groves, Greek hillsides |
| **Aegean blue** | `#1f5fa6` / `#5fa3e0` | the sea, painted temple friezes (pattern only), the Styx's surface by day |
| **Bronze** | `#a0692e` / `#d29a55` | hoplite bronze, Wei bronze, the forge, the automata |
| **Moon white-jade** | `#eef6f2` / `#c9e3d8` | the Moon Palace: white jade halls, the Jade Rabbit's court |
| **Pale moon blue** | `#bcd6f2` / `#8fb4e0` | moonlight on the Moon Court, the night key of the Moon Palace |
| **Nanman sun-bronze** | `#c07a2c` / `#e8b060` | the Nanman south: bronze drums, sun-beaten bronze, feather and bead work |

**The Styx keeps its own colour.** Its water is a deep oath-violet `#3b2a5a` with a faint silver surface sheen `#cfd3e8`. It is never black water and never lit from below, so it can't be mistaken for the Drowned Deep's water.

### The Mandate's sign (reserved object)

**The sign is the Mandate disc** (G1, ruled 2026-10-09: a new object): a palm-sized **pierced jade disc**, a *bi*, flat and round with a round hole at its centre, in pale translucent celadon jade, hung on a **crimson silk cord** threaded through the hole so a woman can wear it, lift it, grab it or tear it away. It is deliberately not the base set's Imperial Jade Seal (`ar-imperial-jade-seal`, a carved block with a dragon top), and the two never appear in the same frame.

- **Its faces are always plain.** It carries no characters, no inscription and no carved figures, only a smooth polished surface with at most a fine raised rim at its outer and inner edges.
- **It glows with one reserved light.** A soft inner glow of heaven-gold `#fff1b0` lives inside the jade. No other object in a Core Set II frame glows with that light, so lamps, braziers, sunsets and halos get their own colour words in the prompt.
- **There is exactly one in any frame.** A Mandate card names who holds it, and where.
- **The size rule.** The disc is palm-sized and the cord is long. At battlefield crop it reads as a bright jade-gold ring on a red line, so it sits at mid-height inside the band, never at the bottom edge.

Three rules for every mention of the sign in a prompt:

1. **Name the bearer and the place:** "the jade Mandate disc on its crimson cord wound round her left fist", never "a glowing jade object" loose in the scene.
2. **Name the light as the disc's own:** "the disc's soft inner heaven-gold glow", and give any other light in the frame its own colour word (lamp amber, brazier orange, sunset rose).
3. **Carry the plain-face clause:** "the disc's faces plain and polished, no characters, no inscription, no carving".

### Marks

A mark is drawn as in section 4b: **biolume cyan `#5ff0e0` / `#aefff6`, reserved**. In this set it is one small pearl of living cyan light set just under the skin, one per mark, countable, at mid-height on the body, with the three 4b rules (a stated count on a named bearer, a state never a transition, the placement clause) on every mark prompt. Cyan appears nowhere else in a Core Set II frame: not in jade, not in the Moon Palace, not in water. Jade is green (`#3f9a78`) and the Moon Court is white and pale blue, and the prompts say so.

### Value floor and lighting doctrine

- **Warm by default.** The default key is a low golden-hour sun from one side, with a cool river-blue or Aegean-blue rim opposite. This is warmer than the base set's upper-left default.
- **About two thirds of frames are daylight (G4).** The rest are night:
  - **Black's night** (the Styx, Jin's intrigues, curses) is lit by a named practical: a lantern, a brazier, or the Styx's silver sheen.
  - **The Moon Court's night** is lit by the full moon as the key, in pale moon blue, with white jade surfaces catching it.
- **The value floor.** The darkest visible value inside the band is deep lacquer-brown `#2b1a17`, and there is no true black. Every illustration carries one surface above roughly 70% luminance inside the band (y 138 to 662): a lit face, marble, a sunlit banner, or white jade.

### NO-TEXT, and the homecoming's variant

The global NO-TEXT rule applies, and this set tests it hardest: Three Kingdoms banners, seals and plaques are where the generators stamp garbled characters.

- **The negative carried on every Core Set II prompt:** `no text, no letters, no numerals, no Chinese characters, no calligraphy, no seal script, no lettered banners, no plaques with writing, no Greek letters, no inscriptions on any surface, no writing on the disc`.
- **Banners are blank or patterned.** House banners carry colour fields, borders and an abstract emblem (Wei's tiger-and-cloud, Wu's flame-wave, Shu's peach blossom, Jin's crane), never a character.
- **Greek friezes are pattern only.** Meander borders and figure-free palmettes, never lettered dedications.

### Composition by mechanic family

The families already in the bible keep their tables, except Whispers, which this set draws its own way (row below):

- 4b for Propagate, mark payoffs and Quest.
- 4c for Tithe's horrors, Skim and Retell.
- 4d for Hunt, Duty, Foresee, and Rage and Warcry.

These are the families new to this set, or drawn differently here:

| Family | The moment to draw |
| --- | --- |
| **Claim the Mandate** | The instant of taking: a hand closing on the crimson cord, the disc swinging at the end of it and catching its own heaven-gold light. When a card claims on arrival, she has just lifted it. When it claims by combat, she tears it from a fallen rival's grasp: the rival is whole and only her hand shows, entering from the frame edge. Never the throne room afterwards. |
| **While you hold the Mandate** | A state: the disc worn openly on its cord at her hip, her breast or her wrist, lit and steady, the woman stronger for it. The disc sits at mid-height. |
| **Whenever you claim it** | A banner, a host or a beast answering the disc, its light thrown across them. |
| **Lose or Nemesis** | The disc held by someone else, out of reach: a cord running out of frame, or an empty hand where the cord was. |
| **Whispers** | A voice made visible: a slow drift of blank leaves or petals leaving her lips toward a listener just past the frame edge, laurel leaves for Greek cards, plum blossom for Three Kingdoms cards. Never water, never the Drowned Deep's drowned gold, never glowing. |
| **Sworn** | **No reserved tell** (brief, section 14). Sworn cards show loyalty in the pose (a knee bent to a legend, a blade raised in salute, an oath cup lifted), but no colour, glow or mark belongs to the mechanic. |
| **Sworn Champions** (the six common legends) | Named heroines drawn as legends in everything but scene: single figure, one idea, the common's two-value background. They wear no crown and no rarity tell, matching the card frame. |
| **Tithe** (non-horror) | The offering made with dignity: the given allies stepping back into light, kneeling and fading to silhouette, or a line of banners lowered. Never bodies, never blood. The card's own subject stands brighter for it. |
| **Sever** | The thread cut: a figure dissolving into drifting ash-silver motes (`#c9ccd6` / `#e6e8ee`) that are not reclaimed, as distinct from death. No gore. |
| **Nine Lives** | The second landing (4a's grammar): the cat-spirit rising from one knee, dust falling, never a death scene. |
| **Mass tap** | The opposing line caught in a held breath (chains of ships, a war-drum's pause, a moon-glare), every figure mid-freeze. |

### The peoples and their tells

**The Three Kingdoms officers** are the house cast as the base set drew them: genderswapped historicals who keep each source character's signature weapon, insignia and colour (global rule). There are no species tells, ever. Each house reads through the base set's costume language:

- **Wei:** lapis lamellar, bronze, black lacquer, the tiger-and-cloud emblem.
- **Wu:** crimson and river-gold, fire and river-fleet imagery, the flame-wave emblem.
- **Shu:** jade and ivory, peach blossom, sworn-brother red cords.
- **Jin:** slate-teal silks, tarnished silver, cranes, court intrigue.
- **The others:** the Han court, the Liang frontier and the Yellow Turbans, each per the base set.

**The Nanman south** (Mulu, Dailai Dongzhu, the war-beasts) wears jungle war-regalia: bronze-scale leather, feather and bead work, and the Nanman sun-bronze accent. Its beasts are plain animals: elephants, tigers and rhinos.

**Greek mortals** (Amazons, hoplites, huntresses, oracles) wear chitons, bronze, and leather pteryges over marble-and-Aegean scenes, per `greek.md`.

**The gods** read divine by light and by the scale of their setting:

- A god is set among vast temples, clouds or the mouth of the Styx.
- Her light is her own: Hephaestus's forge-glow, Nemesis's cold silver, Dionysus's torchlit revel.
- A god is never drawn smaller than a mortal woman, and never as a giant dwarfing a mortal woman in the same frame.

**Nüwa** is drawn as a woman, not a serpent (ruled 2026-10-08), and so are Medusa and every other monster of myth. A monster's nature is carried by at most three stated tells, as Beastkin are.

**Beastkin** follow `beastkin.md`: an adult woman with **at most three stated species tells**, all named in every prompt and at least two of them inside the card window, and tails stated with count, root and tip. Core Set II's Beastkin are Chinese-myth and wild-country Beastkin, not the base set's Japanese yokai register. Costume is handcraft: hemp, felted wool, bamboo, river shell, leather and jade beads. **The Moon Court** (Yutu, the rabbits, Lanlan's otters) wears white jade, pale silk and silver, in the Moon Palace.

The species sheet additions, to be merged into `beastkin.md` at promotion:

| Species (subtype) | Ears | Tail | Locked traits (three tells in all) |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Moon-hare** (Rabbit) | two tall upright rabbit ears, white with pale pink inner ear, never lop | a round white cotton-puff tail | pale ruby-pink eyes. The lop-eared Bunnykin row stays for lop ears |
| **Hound** (Hound) | soft drop ears folded forward | one curled hound tail over the back, one tip | a broad dark hound nose-bridge freckle line |
| **Huli fox** (Fox) | tall triangular fox ears, red-gold with dark backs | one fox brush (Jiuwei: nine, fanned) | narrow amber eyes. Chinese silk costume, never a miko's red-and-white (G3) |
| **Tiger** (Tiger) | rounded tiger ears with a white spot on the back | one long striped tail ending in a black tip | tiger stripes on the forearms and the outer thighs, never the face |
| **Cat** (Cat) | two upright tufted cat ears (as the Nekomata row) | one unforked cat tail, one tip (Maogui: exactly two) | slit vertical pupils |
| **Wolf** (Wolf) | the Wolfkin row | one thick brush wolf tail | amber eyes |
| **Otter** (Otter) | small round ears set low and back | one thick tapering otter tail | webbed fingers (a sleek, wet-looking fur sheen on the forearms may ride with them as description, never as a fourth tell) |
| **Lion-dog** (Lion-Dog) | small rounded ears half hidden in the mane | one plumed tail curling over the back like a fan | a thick curled mane ruff round her neck and shoulders, in tight spiral curls |
| **Seal** (Seal) | no external ears | none (the pelt is the tail) | a spotted grey sealskin mantle worn as a cloak; large dark wet eyes; webbed fingers |
| **Jackal** (Jackal) | tall narrow jackal ears | one bushy jackal tail with a black tip | a dark saddle of fur across the shoulders |
| **Deer** (Deer) | the Deerkin row | short white-flash deer tail | branching antlers |
| **Elk** (Elk) | broad elk ears set low | short pale rump tail | very large many-tined antlers swept back, kept below the headroom line by being swept, never tall |
| **Bear** (Bear) | the Bearkin row | stub tail | heavy claws |
| **Panda** (Panda) | small round black ears | short white stub tail | black eye-patch markings round both eyes, with black fur on the forearms and shoulders as part of the same tell |
| **Holstaur, Boar** | the existing rows | | |

Antlered and horned species follow the headroom rule: antlers count toward the head top, so they are swept back, never towering.

**Constructs** (Bronze Automaton, the Crete sentries, the tomb's clay soldiers) follow `constructs-and-tokens.md`. Hephaestus's automata are golden-bronze women with visible joint seams. The tomb's clay soldiers are terracotta women with a painted-pigment finish, and none of them is drawn after the Qin army's real faces.

## Creatures: Three Kingdoms legends (and Nüwa)

### Jia Nanfeng, the Usurper Empress — `cs2-jia-nanfeng`
- **Card facts:** {3}{B}{R} · B/R (gold frame) · 4/4 · dreaded · ur, legendary · holo: prism · provisional (overplan 2026-10-08)
- **Character & source:** Jia Nanfeng, the Jin empress who seized the court from her husband's regents, drawn as the house cast is: a historical woman, Jin by marriage and Jia Chong's daughter by blood (no family resemblance is bound to the shipped Jia Chong, so none is drawn). The card is three moments at once and the frame takes all three: she claims the Mandate by taking it (the disc torn from a rival's hand), the claim Severs a small enemy (an officer dissolving to ash-silver motes), and while she holds it her host charges (Warcry for the others). The chronicles call her short and dark, so she is drawn that way: thirties, short and compact, sturdy and full-hipped, deep olive-brown skin, thick straight brows, black hair in a low heavy chignon.
- **Personality / mood:** Dreaded: a small woman nobody blocks alone. Cold triumph, no hurry, the palace already hers.
- **Pose & composition:** Seen from a few steps back, head to knees, front three-quarter at the centre of a palace courtyard. Her right fist is raised to chest height with the crimson cord wound round it, the disc swinging just below at about y 410. A rival's hand enters from the left edge at the same height, whole and slender in a slate silk sleeve, fingers just opened and empty. At the right in the mid-ground, one rival guard is dissolving into ash-silver motes from the feet up, halberd still upright. Behind her, the halberds and blank crimson banners of her own guard surge forward, caught in the disc's light. Face about y 250, eye line y 270. Head top at or below y 179.
- **Costume & attire:** Lacquer-red empress robe over slate-teal underlayers, tarnished-silver phoenix pattern woven into the hem and cuffs (pattern only), a high collar, a wide plum-black sash, a flat tarnished-silver phoenix pin lying low in the chignon.
- **Palette:** Gold frame `#e8c95a` / `#7a5a18`, accent `#fff2b8`; B `#5a3a70` / `#140d1c`, accent `#9b6fc4`; R `#d95436` / `#5e0f0f`, accent `#f7b267`; lacquer red `#b3202a` / `#e0473c`; Jin slate-teal and tarnished silver; pale celadon jade disc with heaven-gold `#fff1b0` inner glow; ash-silver motes `#c9c6c0`.
- **Lighting:** Black's night, lit by a practical: a row of hanging palace lanterns at the right as the one lamp-amber key; a cool river-blue `#7fb3de` night rim on her left edge and along the dissolving guard.
- **Expression:** Chin lowered, a thin closed-mouth smile, eyes on the viewer as if the viewer were next.
- **Props / weapon:** The jade Mandate disc on its crimson cord (the secondary canon element), a short jian sheathed at her hip; the rival's empty hand; the Severed guard.
- **Background:** The inner court of the Luoyang palace at night: lacquer-red pillars, grey stone paving, lantern rows, a dark roofline against a deep blue sky.
- **Holo interaction:** Prism: the disc at the frame's centre is the glow the rings breathe from; keep her face and the plum sash smooth.
- **Rarity ambition:** Ultra rare box-art: the usurpation in one frame, the take, the cost, the host.
- **Prompt:** EXACTLY ONE adult woman in full view plus one rival woman's hand at the left edge and one rival guard dissolving at the right, historical Jin empress Jia Nanfeng, seen from a few steps back, her figure from head to knees inside the middle band, not a close-up, front three-quarter at the centre of a night palace courtyard, short and compact, sturdy and full-hipped, in her thirties, ### Lady Bian, Matriarch of Wei — `cs2-lady-bian`
- **Card facts:** {2}{W}{B} · W/B (gold frame) · 3/5 · bloodoath · ur, legendary · holo: prism · provisional (overplan 2026-10-08)
- **Character & source:** Lady Bian, Cao Cao's empress and Cao Pi's mother, the singer who became the dynasty's conscience: the legend Wei's sworn officers die for, and every death feeds her. Sworn has no reserved tell, so loyalty is the pose (three officers kneeling to her, oath cups raised). Her drain is drawn as the hall's memorial lamps, one lit for each officer already given, their warm light gathering on her. Forties, tall and full-figured, warm ivory skin, the amber eyes her son inherited, dark brown hair with first grey at the temples in a low coiled chignon.
- **Personality / mood:** Grave, warm and unbending; grief carried as authority, gratitude as a weapon.
- **Pose & composition:** Front three-quarter, knees up, standing at the head of the ancestral hall's steps at the centre, a bronze oath cup raised at shoulder height in her right hand, her left hand open toward the officers. Below her in the mid-ground, three Wei officers kneel in a row, heads bowed and lower than her waist, each lifting a small oath cup. Along the hall's side wall at the left, a row of five lit memorial lamps on plain blank tablets. Face about y 255, eye line y 275. Head top at or below y 179.
- **Costume & attire:** Ivory silk court robe under a plum-black overrobe woven with a lapis tiger-and-cloud pattern, bronze clasps at the shoulders, a wide lapis sash, a single bronze hairpin laid flat.
- **Palette:** Gold frame `#e8c95a` / `#7a5a18`, accent `#fff2b8`; W `#f2e8cf` / `#c9a84c`; B `#5a3a70`, accent `#9b6fc4`; Wei lapis and bronze `#a0692e` / `#d29a55`; imperial gold `#d4a83a` trims; lamp amber in the memorial lamps.
- **Lighting:** Low golden-hour sun through the hall's open doors behind her left shoulder as the one warm key; a cool lapis-blue dusk rim on her right side and the officers' helmets.
- **Expression:** Level, moist-eyed, unsmiling; the look of a woman accepting a debt she will collect.
- **Props / weapon:** The bronze oath cup; the three kneeling officers (the secondary canon element); the five lamps.
- **Background:** The Wei ancestral hall at Ye at dusk: dark lacquer pillars, bronze braziers, blank tiger-and-cloud banners, the sunset through open doors.
- **Holo interaction:** Prism: the sun through the doors just off-centre behind her shoulder is the glow; keep the plum overrobe and her face smooth, put texture in the lapis weave.
- **Rarity ambition:** Ultra rare box-art: the matriarch and the oath that kills for her.

## Creatures: Greek legends

### Hebe, Cupbearer of Olympus — `cs2-hebe`
- **Card facts:** {2}{W}{W} · W · 3/5 · sentinel · ur, legendary · holo: prism · provisional (overplan 2026-10-08)
- **Character & source:** Hebe as-is, cupbearer of the gods, who pours the ambrosia at Olympus's table. She replaces the base set's Hestia as white's UR and is the life-gain decks' Sworn enabler: her arrival pours 4 life, and at Sunset, if life was gained, she marks a creature you control. The art draws both in one sunset: the cup filled, and the drinker already marked. Mid-twenties and plainly adult, soft full-figured build, warm ivory-peach skin, honey-blonde curls bound low at the nape with a gilt fillet and small white blossoms, warm brown-gold eyes. She reads divine by the cloud-terrace and the sunset, never by scale. Kept apart from `gk-hestia`: no veil, no hearth, no brazier.
- **Personality / mood:** Generous, bright, unhurried: the host who notices whose cup is empty. Sentinel: she pours without ever leaving her watch.
- **Pose & composition:** Seen from a few steps back, head to knees inside the band. Hebe stands upright in front three-quarter, left of centre, on the top step of a marble cloud-terrace. Her right hand tips a tall gilt oinochoe and pours a ribbon of honey-amber ambrosia into a wide kylix. Exactly one adult Sworn warrior woman kneels on one knee on the step to her right and holds the cup up in both hands. The kneeler's head sits at about y 330, lower than Hebe's. Exactly one cyan pearl sits under the skin below the kneeler's left collarbone, at about y 400. Hebe's face is at about y 260, with her eye line at y 270, looking down at the drinker. ### Ariadne, Mistress of the Labyrinth — `cs2-ariadne`
- **Card facts:** {2}{U}{G} · U/G (gold frame) · 2/4 · — · ssr, legendary · holo: shiny · provisional (overplan 2026-10-08)
- **Character & source:** Ariadne as-is, princess of Crete and mistress of the Labyrinth. Her kit is the clew: the ball of thread that finds the way through, which is also the secondary canon element. Her Duty (Foresee 2) is drawn as the act of tending: the thread unwinding as she reads the path ahead. Her arrival (a land from the Warchest) is drawn where the thread ends: a walled green garden court of olive trees and spring water, newly opened at the maze's heart. Mid-twenties, slender and graceful, light olive skin, long wavy black hair with Minoan curls at the brow and the long fall bound low at the nape, dark eyes. A slim star-rosette circlet foreshadows her myth's crown, worn low.
- **Personality / mood:** Clever, quietly daring, poised. She knows the maze better than its builder.
- **Pose & composition:** Seen from a few steps back, head to knees, front three-quarter, right of centre, on the rampart walk above the Labyrinth. Her left hand holds the clew at waist height. Her right hand pays out a bright red-gold thread that runs down over the parapet and threads the maze's turns to a far opening into the green court, at about y 450. ### Nanman War-Elephant — `cs2-nanman-war-elephant`
- **Card facts:** {4}{G}{G} · G · 6/6 · overrun, dreaded · ssr · holo: shiny · provisional (overplan 2026-10-08)
- **Character & source:** A plain Nanman war-elephant, the south's top end: a plain animal with no species tells, driven by an unnamed Nanman mahout woman on its neck (the card is a Beast, so the handler is a supporting figure, not the card's subject, and she is deliberately not Mulu; see flags). The elephant matches Mulu's mount in her legend entry: ash-grey, with a sun-bronze brow plate. Overrun is the verb: it bursts through an enemy palisade. Dreaded: the abandoned spears and toppled shields of a line that broke before it arrived. The mahout: thirties, broad-shouldered and muscular, very dark brown skin, black hair in tight close braids bound low at the nape.
- **Personality / mood:** An avalanche with a driver. Nothing in front of it stays standing; the mahout is grinning.
- **Pose & composition:** Extreme wide shot, crown to feet, nothing cropped. The elephant charges three-quarter toward lower-left through a splintering timber palisade, forefoot raised mid-stride, trunk curled forward and low. The mahout sits astride its neck behind the head, leaning forward, goad raised at shoulder height. Her head top at or below y 179 and the highest point of the whole group; the elephant's brow plate below her chin. - **Lighting:** Low golden-hour sun from the right as key across the elephant's flank and the mahout's face; cool river-blue `#7fb3de` rim along the elephant's back and ears from the open sky behind.
- **Expression:** Mahout: wide fierce grin, eyes on the next wall. Elephant: small eye narrowed, ears flared.
- **Props / weapon:** A bronze-hooked ankus goad; the splintering palisade; abandoned spears and shields scattered at the elephant's knee height on a raised bank.
- **Background:** A jungle river-valley fort at golden hour, bronze war-drums on a far ridge, dust and splinters in the air.

### Wei Muster-Captain — `cs2-wei-muster-captain`
- **Card facts:** {2}{W} · W · 1/4 · sentinel · sr · holo: sheen · provisional (overplan 2026-10-08)
- **Character & source:** An original Wei officer of the ranks, the Empower cameo: pay and every creature you control grows. Drawn as the muster itself: her signal raised, the drilled ranks below answering in one motion, their armour flashing brighter in the sun than a moment before (4b's Empower, a line running brighter than it was built for). Sentinel: she watches from the wall, she does not charge. Mid-forties, tall and broad-shouldered, fair pale skin with sun-creases at the eyes, iron-grey hair in a low tight bun.
- **Personality / mood:** Thirty years of shouting the same order and it still works. Dry, proud, unhurried.
- **Pose & composition:** She stands at the left third on a rammed-earth rampart walk, three-quarter right, seen head to knees, her right arm raising a bronze command baton with a short blank lapis pennant level with her shoulder. Below and behind her to the right, a courtyard of small, distant soldier women in ordered rows raise their spears in unison. Head top at or below y 179; face ≈ y 270, eye line ≈ y 290.
- **Costume & attire:** Lapis-lacquered lamellar over a long ivory officer's robe, one bronze pauldron cast with the Wei tiger-and-cloud emblem in relief, a short campaign cloak, a plain bronze hairpin.
- **Palette:** W `#f2e8cf` / `#c9a84c`, accent `#fffef2`; Wei lapis `#2f6fa8` and bronze `#a0692e` / `#d29a55`; imperial gold `#d4a83a` on the baton.
- **Lighting:** Low golden-hour sun from the left as key on her face and the ranks' spear points; cool river-blue `#7fb3de` rim along her shoulder from the evening sky.
- **Expression:** Mouth open mid-command, eyes narrowed and satisfied.
- **Props / weapon:** The bronze command baton with its blank pennant; a sheathed jian at her hip.
- **Background:** A Wei camp courtyard inside rammed-earth walls, watchtowers, blank lapis banners patterned with the tiger-and-cloud emblem.
- **Holo interaction:** Sheen: the sweep runs across the ranks' raised spear points and her lacquer; keep the courtyard ground toned, not flat white.

### Bronze Automaton of the Forge — `cs2-bronze-automaton`
- **Card facts:** {5} · C · 4/5 · sentinel · ssr · holo: shiny · provisional (overplan 2026-10-08)
- **Character & source:** An original handmaiden of Hephaestus's forge on Lemnos, drawn to the register's construct rule: a woman cast whole in golden bronze, with visible joint seams at the neck, shoulders, elbows, wrists, waist and knees. She is tall and statuesque, broad-shouldered and full-hipped, with an athlete's cast proportions, a smooth polished face and warm amber enamel eyes. Sentinel is her open, watchful gaze while she works. Her Duty taps a creature, so she is drawn mid-act, holding something fast with a chain.
- **Personality / mood:** Patient, tireless and faintly gentle: the keeper who never sleeps and never lets go.
- **Pose & composition:** Seen from a few steps back, head to knees, at the left-centre in three-quarter view facing right. Her feet are braced wide on the forge floor and she leans back against the pull, both hands on a heavy bronze chain that runs taut out of the right edge at waist height (about y 450), one turn wound round her left forearm. Her head turns to look along the chain with her eyes wide open. Eye line about y 300; head top at or below y 179, the highest point of the figure.
- **Costume & attire:** Her body is the costume: bronze plates cast as a short pleated chiton and a breastplate with meander borders (pattern only), close sculpted bronze waves of hair bound low at the nape, and a gilt laurel band cast flat to the hair. Each seam is a thin dark line with a faint forge-orange glimmer inside.
- **Palette:** C `#a9adb5` / `#4e535c`, accent `#dfe3ea` in the ash and stone; bronze `#a0692e` / `#d29a55` and imperial gold `#d4a83a` / `#f5d77a` on her body; forge orange in the coals; marble white `#f4f1ea` columns; Aegean blue `#5fa3e0` sea; darkest soot `#2b1a17`.
- **Lighting:** Key: forge-orange firelight from the great hearth at the left, raking the polished bronze. Rim: cool Aegean-blue daylight from the open colonnade at the right, a clean edge down her back.
- **Expression:** Calm and intent, lips closed in a small faint smile, eyes fixed along the chain.
- **Props / weapon:** The bronze chain; an anvil and a great forge hammer at rest behind her.
- **Background:** Hephaestus's forge-hall on Lemnos: smoke-browned stone, a hearth of forge-orange coals, racks of unfinished bronze limbs, an open colonnade onto a sunlit Aegean sea and a volcanic slope.
- **Holo interaction:** Shiny: the sweep runs down the bronze plates and chain links; keep the face plate softly polished, the sunlit opening textured with sea haze.
- **Rarity ambition:** Double super rare moment: a made woman doing her duty in her maker's hall, holding fast something we never see.

### Shade of the Asphodel Fields — `cs2-shade-of-asphodel`
- **Card facts:** {2}{B} · B · 3/2 · — · sr · holo: sheen · provisional (overplan 2026-10-08)
- **Character & source:** An original shade of the underworld's Asphodel Fields, where the ordinary dead wander. She is slim, in her mid-thirties, with long loose dark-brown hair threaded with silver. Her skin was warm olive in life and is now a cool, desaturated grey-olive. The Shade's tell is her lower legs and hem dissolving into thin silver-grey mist, with no feet. Preserve is drawn by the Duat grammar: one translucent after-image of her own silhouette, half-overlapping her, an echo and not another person. Here the echo is pale asphodel-white.
- **Personality / mood:** Wistful and unhurried, a woman who remembers the sun and walks toward it anyway.
- **Pose & composition:** Waist-deep in asphodel, three-quarter left at the centre, she drifts forward holding a small clay oil lamp at chest height. Her pale echo is one half-step ahead, half-overlapping her and turned the same way, its outstretched hand already further forward than hers. Eye line about y 310; head top at or below y 179.
- **Costume & attire:** A faded violet peplos pinned at both shoulders with plain bronze fibulae, a grey himation slipping off one arm, bare arms, a thin silver anklet lost in the mist.
- **Palette:** B `#5a3a70` / `#140d1c` (shadow lifted to `#2b1a17`), accent `#9b6fc4`; asphodel white `#f4f1ea` / `#d9d3c5` flowers and echo; lamp amber in the flame; Styx silver `#cfd3e8` haze.
- **Lighting:** Key: lamp-amber light from the oil lamp in her hand, warm on her face and the near flowers. Rim: cool silver-violet twilight from the endless sky behind.
- **Expression:** Soft, distant, lips parted as if about to say a name.
- **Props / weapon:** The clay oil lamp; no weapon.
- **Background:** The Asphodel Fields: an endless meadow of pale asphodel under a starless violet-grey twilight, faint shades walking far off, a low ridge of grey poplars.
- **Holo interaction:** Sheen: the sweep slides across the pale flowers and the echo; keep the meadow textured with petals so it never whites out.
- **Rarity ambition:** Super rare moment: the copy stepping out ahead of the dead woman.

- **Card facts:** {2}{R} · R · 2/3 · — · sr · holo: sheen · provisional (overplan 2026-10-08)
- **Character & source:** An original Greek hero of the beachhead below Troy, athletic and long-limbed, late twenties, sun-tanned Anatolian olive skin, dark curly hair tied in a low tail. Her card awakens (+2/+1 and Twin Blades) whenever you claim the Mandate. She is drawn by the register's "whenever you claim it" row: the jade Mandate disc on its crimson cord is newly wound round her own left hand, and its light is thrown across her as she draws her second sword.
- **Personality / mood:** A good soldier becoming a legend in one breath: astonished, then certain.
- **Pose & composition:** Front three-quarter facing left, head to knees, at the centre-right. Her right hand already holds a bronze xiphos low. The jade Mandate disc swings free on a hand's length of cord below the fist, at mid-height (about y 380), and its heaven-gold light falls up across her face and both blades. Eye line about y 300, glancing down at the disc; head top at or below y 179.

- **Expression:** Wide-eyed wonder turning to resolve, lips parted on a breath.
- **Props / weapon:** Two bronze leaf-bladed xiphe; the jade Mandate disc on its crimson cord, wound round her left hand.

Tithe is drawn by the register's dignified, non-horror row: two lesser shades kneel behind her and fade to silhouette into the Styx's silver sheen, and she stands brighter for it. Deathblade is one quiet lethal implement.

- **Props / weapon:** The dark bronze harpe; the two braziers.
- **Background:** The bank of the Styx before the bronze gates of Tartarus: still oath-violet water with a silver surface sheen, huge bronze doors ajar in a cliff of brown basalt.

## Flags from drafting

Each writer listed the art-direction questions the register did not answer. Each was drafted on the writer's best version; the owner's answers become register lines.

### Three Kingdoms legends (and Nüwa)

- **Yu Ji (`cs2-yu-ji`):** RULED 2026-10-09 (G5): Whispers has the set's own look; the entry is redrawn.
- **Lady Gan (`cs2-lady-gan`):** RULED 2026-10-09 (G8): no infant.
- **Mulu (`cs2-mulu`):** her mount is drawn as a plain ash-grey war-elephant with a sun-bronze brow plate, bronze-capped tusks and harness bells (the Romance's elephant is white). The `cs2-nanman-war-elephant` entry ("Mulu's mount") belongs to another batch and should match one design. Confirm ash-grey or white, and bind it in section 7 at promotion.
- **Jia Nanfeng (`cs2-jia-nanfeng`):** she is drawn short, compact and dark-skinned, following the chronicles' description, as a directed body choice. Confirm that read for an R29 tower portrait.
- **Nüwa (`cs2-nuwa`):** the five banners lowered on the slope below are my reading of non-horror Tithe at five. Her marks are drawn as five pearls on one forearm, at mid-height. Confirm a single row is the right way to show the five stones.

### Greek legends

- **Hephaestus (`cs2-hephaestus`):** her myth's lameness is kept as a worked gilt-bronze brace on her left shin and knee, drawn like jewellery and strength. Keep it, or drop it as a disability tell the card does not need?
- **Hippolyta (`cs2-hippolyta`):** she wears the queen's war-girdle, drafted as a broad gilt-bronze belt of linked crescent plaques. The separate artifact Girdle of the Amazon Queen (`cs2-girdle-of-hippolyta`, batch H) should show the same object. Which entry sets the design?
- **The Amazon royal house (Hippolyta, Penthesilea, Antiope):** drafted with a shared resemblance (grey-green eyes, olive skin, auburn-to-copper hair, a crescent pelta motif) and distinct builds and hair, like the base set's family entries. Add it to the section 7 canon registry at promotion?
- **Circe (`cs2-circe`):** her art defines the 1/1 Pig token's look (a small round pink domestic pig with tan patches, a curly tail and a bewildered face). Make it a registry token entry so the token art matches? The card notes also leave a real "becomes a pig" transform as an open VOCAB question. The art reads as one either way.
- **Cassandra (`cs2-cassandra`):** RULED 2026-10-09 (G5): Whispers has the set's own look; the entry is redrawn.
- **Medusa (`cs2-medusa`):** her three tells are snake hair, serpent-gold slit-pupil eyes, and jade scales at the collarbones and forearms (no wings, no tail, no fangs). Her gaze is drawn as the target's weapons and shield turned to stone while the body stays whole, plus a garden of whole, intact statues of warrior women. Is that the right line, or should the frozen target stay entirely flesh?
- **Hebe (`cs2-hebe`):** her gilt two-handled cup may want to match the separate artifact Ambrosia Cup of Olympus (`cs2-ambrosia-cup`, batch H). Same object, or deliberately different?

### Beastkin legends, then Beastkin creatures

- **Cerberus (`cs2-cerberus`), G2:** RULED 2026-10-09: three hound-women bound by one chain, one per colour; the entry is redrawn.
- **Twice-Tailed Ghost-Cat (`cs2-ghost-cat`):** the register's Cat row says one tail (Maogui: exactly two), but this card is named "Twice-Tailed". I drafted exactly two tails. Should the Cat row's exception read "Maogui and the Twice-Tailed Ghost-Cat: exactly two", or should the card be renamed and drawn with one tail?
- **Otter tells (Lanlan, Otter Ferry-Runner, River-Court Pearl Diver):** the register's Otter row lists ears, tail and two more traits (webbed fingers; wet fur sheen on the forearms), which makes four. I drew the two as one tell ("webbed fingers with sleek wet-looking otter fur on the backs of the hands and forearms") to keep three. Please confirm, so the other Beastkin writer's otters match.
- **Zhuxiong (`cs2-zhuxiong`):** RULED 2026-10-09: she is a Panda; the entry is redrawn.
- **River-Court Pearl Diver (`cs2-river-court-diver`):** an otter with Skyborne has no wings, so I drew her riding a great white crane, low over the river. Is a mount acceptable, or would you prefer another way to show flight (for example a leap from a waterfall, which reads less clearly as flying)?
- **Howler of the Wolf Pack (`cs2-wolf-pack-howler`):** the Wolf token is drawn to match the canon `tok-wolf` dire-wolf silhouette (grey, hackled fur). Does Core Set II's 2/2 Wolf token reuse `tok-wolf`, or does it get its own token entry?
- **Jiuwei (`cs2-jiuwei`), G3 (open):** drafted on the register's draft position: nine tails shared with the Kitsune row, told apart by hanfu silk and jade with "not a miko, no red-and-white shrine robe" in the prompt.

### Beastkin creatures (continued)

- **Otter Reed-Diver, Moon-Otter Skipper, Otter Pearl-Thief (Otter row).** The register's Otter row lists ears, tail, webbed fingers and a sleek wet fur sheen on the forearms: four features against a cap of three. These entries treat "webbed fingers with a sleek, wet-looking fur sheen on the forearms" as one hands-and-forearms tell. Confirm, or name which one to drop. The legends writer (Lanlan, Otter Ferry-Runner, River-Court Pearl Diver) should match whatever is ruled.
- **Holstaur Herdswoman (Holstaur row).** The existing row has four features (ears, tail, horns, black-and-white hide markings), and the base set also uses a neck-bell. Drafted with ears, horns and tail, leaving out the hide markings and the bell. Is that the right three?
- **Boar-Clan Charger (Boarkin row).** The existing row has four features (ears, tail, tusks, bristle-mane ridge). Drafted with ears, tusks and tail, leaving out the mane ridge. Is that the right three?
- **Japanese cat names (RULED 2026-10-09, G7):** renamed to Blood-Sipper of Jinhua, Maoyao Lantern-Lighter and Hollow-Eyed Temple Cat.
- **Blood-Sipper of Jinhua.** Blood Oath is drawn as a sip of dark plum wine with a warm flush, with no blood at all. Is that OK, or do you want a more predatory read without gore?
- **Panda Grove-Keeper.** The register's Panda row puts two features in one tell: "black eye-patch markings round both eyes, and black fur on the forearms and shoulders". Drafted as one marking tell. Its mark goes on the forearm of an unseen ally (only the forearm shows). The alternative is the Panda marking herself, which keeps to a strict single figure but loses the card's "another creature" verb.
- **Night share.** 12 of the 34 frames are night: the 7 Moon Court frames and 5 black frames. Jackal Grave-Digger and Otter Pearl-Thief are set at sunset, and Moon-Rabbit Courier is moved to dawn, to keep near the G4 two-thirds daylight target.

### Three Kingdoms creatures

- **Nanman War-Elephant** (`cs2-nanman-war-elephant`): its role note calls it "Mulu's mount". At the coordinator's consistency note the elephant now matches Mulu's entry (ash-grey, with a sun-bronze brow plate), but its rider is still an unnamed Nanman mahout, because Mulu's own look is set in the legends batch. Should the rider be Mulu in her canon look (to be matched at merge), or stay an anonymous handler?
- **Herald of Heaven's Mandate** (`cs2-herald-of-heaven`): a mortal Human herald with Skyborne. She is drawn borne on trailing silk ribbons, in the style of a celestial dancer, with no wings. Is ribbon flight acceptable for a Human, or should she ride a mount (a crane, as the Jin Chronicler does)?
- **Lantern-Boat Smuggler** (`cs2-lantern-boat-smuggler`): Skyborne is drawn as a skiff lifted by six sky-lanterns. This is an invented conceit, not a historical device. Is that whimsy in register for the homecoming?
- **Nanman War-Elephant Calf** (`cs2-nanman-elephant-calf`): drawn beast-alone (no handler), so the calf reads as the card's whole subject at common. Its crown is pinned to "one third down" rather than the quarter-line clause, because a beast-alone animal at this scale sits lower in the frame. Should a handler be added, to match the Double Super Rare elephant's mahout?

### Greek creatures

- **Echo of the Sibyl** (`cs2-echo-of-the-sibyl`): RULED 2026-10-09 (G5): Whispers has the set's own look; the entry is redrawn.
- **Lamia of the Ruined Shrine** (`cs2-lamia`): the register's monster rule (Medusa and every monster of myth drawn as a woman) contradicts `beastkin.md`'s Serpent/lamia row, which gives a serpent lower body. Drafted as a woman with three tells and two human legs. Should the beastkin row note that Core Set II's mythic Lamia is the exception, or should the row itself change?
- **The Shade tell** (`cs2-shade-of-asphodel`, `cs2-shade-of-tartarus`, `cs2-shade-of-lethe`): all three Shades share one type tell, their lower legs and hem dissolving into silver-grey mist with no feet, and a desaturated skin. Confirm this as the set's Shade look, so that Persephone's and the Styx cards' entries match it.
- **Huntress of the Moonlit Glade** (`cs2-moonlit-huntress`): the card name demands night, but G4 keeps night for black and the Moon Court. Drafted moonlit, in cool silver-white rather than the Moon Court's pale moon blue. Is a green night frame acceptable, or should it be a dusk frame?

### Charms and rituals

- **The Pillars of Heaven Fall** (`cs2-pillars-fall`) and the other destroy cards: the register says how to draw Sever (ash-silver motes that are not reclaimed) but not how to draw a plain destroy, so the two can be told apart. I drew destroy as figures driven down, sinking under a surface, or fading to a silhouette, and I never used ash-silver motes for it. Should a plain destroy get its own reserved look?
- **Sever motes hex:** the register gives no hex for Sever's ash-silver. I used `#c9ccd6` / `#e6e8ee` on all three Sever cards (`cs2-heavens-edict`, `cs2-banished-frontier`, `cs2-judgment-of-hades`). Promote these, or name others?
- **Shipped characters named in card titles** (`cs2-judgment-of-hades`, `cs2-arrow-of-artemis`, `cs2-demeter-bounty`, and the Zhuge Liang stratagems `cs2-straw-boats` / `cs2-empty-fort`): I kept the shipped goddess or officer out of frame, or used an unnamed follower with no signature kit (no bident, no crescent diadem, no feather fan), so the spell can't contradict canon. Should these spells show the shipped character instead?
- **The Otter row of the species sheet** (`cs2-otters-slip`) lists four traits: ears, tail, webbed fingers and a wet fur sheen on the forearms. That breaks the three-tell cap. I used ears, tail and webbed fingers. Which three are locked?
- **Ivory-white `#fffef2` and heaven-gold `#fff1b0`** sit very close in value. On `cs2-pillars-fall` the W ward light and the Mandate disc share a frame. I called the pillar's light "ivory-white and never gold". It's worth a QA look at the first render.

### Enchantments and artifacts

- **Curse-Doll of Yu Ji** (`cs2-yu-ji-curse-doll`): drawn as her hands and sleeves only, so the artifact cannot contradict the Yu Ji legend's own entry in batch A. Should the legend appear in full instead, once her design is settled?
- **Seal of the Fallen Court** (`cs2-fallen-court-seal`): the card's own seal is drawn as a square tarnished bronze seal with a tortoise knob, its face turned down, warmed by the Mandate disc's light ("whenever you claim it"). Now that the sign is a disc, a bronze seal block is clearly a different object. Is a seal block acceptable in a Mandate frame, or should the "seal of the fallen court" become another court object, such as a bronze tiger tally?
- **The Peach Garden Oath** (`cs2-peach-garden-oath`): drawn as three unnamed Shu sworn sisters, not as the three Shu legends. Should it show the legends instead?
- **Antler Crown of the Deer-Kin** (`cs2-antler-crown`): worn by a Wolf Beastkin, not a Deer Beastkin, so the crown does not stack on a Deer-kin's own antlers. Is that the right bearer?
